{"parentUuid":"53025837-a92c-4dba-af41-661ebfc4ed03","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01KjrbqXDqAdu2RtyPDXN6aL","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_017BUsDAE4tMioJLvFeNs4kj","name":"Glob","input":{"pattern":".claude/agents/*.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":27636,"cache_read_input_tokens":57966,"output_tokens":2203,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":27636,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[{"input_tokens":1,"output_tokens":2203,"cache_read_input_tokens":57966,"cache_creation_input_tokens":27636,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":27636},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CaY7ZkFzNsukxLEuemsYV","type":"assistant","uuid":"e5bcc915-f4f8-40f9-bece-9d872f893ced","timestamp":"2026-04-29T13:07:30.458Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"45ff2d8f-d633-45f8-8a6e-c5fb5ce08842","version":"2.1.122","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"53025837-a92c-4dba-af41-661ebfc4ed03","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"fc11436c-fada-41bc-8ae7-32ac3f984211","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YJSJv8b2er4VapnDxwMihT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: decompose-work\n3\tdescription: Split a non-trivial task into deterministic-vs-LLM portions before execution. Use when planning multi-step work where some sub-tasks are mechanical (file ops, regex, count, schema validation) and others require judgment (synthesis, design, ambiguity resolution). Outputs structured plan that maps each portion to the cheapest competent tool. Pairs with capability-map.md (model × effort × task_class grounding) and try-n-approaches (S9 — Karpathy autoresearch loop).\n4\tallowed-tools: [Read, Glob, Grep, Bash]\n5\t---\n6\t\n7\t# Skill: decompose-work\n8\t\n9\t## Purpose\n10\t\n11\tKarpathy autoresearch loop foundation: BEFORE doing a task, **classify sub-parts as deterministic (cheap, cacheable, verifiable) vs LLM-required (judgment, ambiguous, generative)**. Mechanical work goes to scripts; LLM cycles go to where judgment is irreducible. Avoids the two failure modes: (a) burning LLM tokens on `wc -l` style work that bash does for free, (b) hand-coding logic that should be a one-paragraph judgment call.\n12\t\n13\tPer UP-06 §3 verbatim: *\"cần phải có một skill hoặc agent với khả năng chuyên biệt giúp nó 'bóc tách' được ra thế nào là 'deterministic' và thế nào là 'llm probability'\"*.\n14\t\n15\t## When to Use\n16\t\n17\t- Task has ≥3 distinct sub-parts\n18\t- Some sub-parts smell mechanical, others smell judgment-heavy\n19\t- Estimated context cost ≥30K if done naively LLM-only\n20\t- About to dispatch subagent — knowing the deterministic floor saves token budget\n21\t- Authoring a new skill or hook — decomposition surfaces what belongs in the script vs prompt\n22\t\n23\t## When NOT to Use\n24\t\n25\t- Task is trivial (≤2 steps, single tool call)\n26\t- Task is purely deterministic (just run the script directly)\n27\t- Task is purely judgment-only with no measurable outputs (e.g., \"draft this paragraph\" — LLM end-to-end)\n28\t- Already mid-execution and re-decomposing would just be ceremonious\n29\t\n30\t## Inputs\n31\t\n32\t| Arg | Required | Purpose |\n33\t|---|---|---|\n34\t| `task_description` | yes | Free-form text describing the goal |\n35\t| `--budget` | no | Approximate token budget (helps prioritize deterministic offload) |\n36\t| `--depth` | no | `shallow` (3-5 portions) \\| `deep` (full sub-tree). Default shallow. |\n37\t\n38\t## Process\n39\t\n40\t1. **Parse task** — list candidate sub-parts. Quote verbatim phrases from `task_description`. Don't paraphrase yet — paraphrase loses signal.\n41\t2. **Read `agent-workspace/memory/capability-map.md`** if exists — note which cells (model × effort × task_class) match this task's flavor. If map empty/missing, proceed without grounding (note in output).\n42\t3. **Classify each sub-part** per `references/classification-heuristics.md`:\n43\t   - **DETERMINISTIC** if: file-search/glob, line/byte/match count, regex extract, JSON/YAML parse, schema validate, format conversion, deterministic transformation, hash, sort, dedupe, binary diff\n44\t   - **LLM-REQUIRED** if: synthesis across sources, design/naming, ambiguity arbitration, free-text generation, judgment under uncertainty, calibration interpretation, multi-perspective adversarial reasoning\n45\t   - **HYBRID** if: deterministic skeleton + LLM interpretation step, OR LLM drives deterministic tool use, OR threshold-based escalation (e.g., \"auto-classify if confidence ≥0.8 else escalate to LLM\")\n46\t4. **For each portion, propose the cheapest competent tool**:\n47\t   - Deterministic → bash/grep/awk/node oneliner, hook script, or existing tool (Glob, Grep, Bash, Read)\n48\t   - LLM → main session, subagent (specify which: opus/sonnet/haiku, fresh-ctx?), or skill\n49\t   - Hybrid → deterministic gate + escalation path\n50\t5. **Build integration plan** — sequence + data flow + escalation rules. Where does deterministic output feed LLM input? Where does LLM decision drive deterministic action? Where's the failure escape?\n51\t6. **Surface risks** — what if classification is wrong? What if LLM portion exceeds budget? What's the fallback if deterministic step fails?\n52\t7. **Output structured result** per `references/output-template.md`:\n53\t   ```\n54\t   ## Decomposition: <task name>\n55\t   ### Deterministic portions (N)\n56\t   - [D-1] sub-task → tool → expected output\n57\t   ### LLM-required portions (N)\n58\t   - [L-1] sub-task → agent/skill → why LLM needed\n59\t   ### Hybrid portions (N)\n60\t   - [H-1] sub-task → gate + escalation\n61\t   ### Integration plan\n62\t   1. ... 2. ... 3. ...\n63\t   ### Risks + fallbacks\n64\t   - ...\n65\t   ### Capability-map grounding\n66\t   - cells consulted: ... | gaps: ...\n67\t   ```\n68\t\n69\t## Validation Pre-Conditions\n70\t\n71\t- `task_description` is non-empty + ≥1 sentence\n72\t- Skill output has ≥1 deterministic portion (if everything LLM, the skill isn't earning its keep — recommend skipping)\n73\t- Integration plan covers ALL portions (no orphan sub-tasks)\n74\t\n75\t## Anti-Patterns\n76\t\n77\t- **Treating \"code generation\" as fully deterministic** — design naming + interface choice is LLM. Only the resulting build/lint/test step is deterministic.\n78\t- **Over-decomposing trivial tasks** — `cat foo` doesn't need this skill.\n79\t- **Skipping integration plan** — listing parts without sequencing them is half a deliverable.\n80\t- **Ignoring capability-map** — historical cells encode \"where LLM has hit limits\"; skipping them re-discovers those limits the hard way.\n81\t- **Confusing \"LLM-required\" with \"complex\"** — a simple-feeling task with ambiguity (e.g., \"rename this thing well\") is LLM-required even if short.\n82\t- **Decomposing AFTER execution** — that's a postmortem, not a decomposer; this skill is pre-execution.\n83\t\n84\t## Smoke Test (S8 first run)\n85\t\n86\tSample task: *\"Author Track 5.5c.6 promote-rule skill that scans agent-notes for clusterable rules and proposes promotion to hook/skill/charter.\"*\n87\t\n88\tExpected decomposition (target):\n89\t- Deterministic: read agent-notes.md (Read), regex-extract rule headers (Grep), token-similarity heuristic (bash + node), output proposal markdown (Write)\n90\t- LLM-required: cluster naming, promotion-target choice (hook vs skill vs charter), prevention-rule rephrasing\n91\t- Hybrid: similarity threshold gate (deterministic) → if cluster ≥3, LLM evaluates whether promotion is warranted\n92\t\n93\tIf smoke test produces this rough shape, skill is functional.\n94\t\n95\t## See Also\n96\t\n97\t- `agent-workspace/memory/capability-map.md` — model × effort × task_class living grounding\n98\t- `.claude/skills/promote-rule/SKILL.md` — sibling 5.5c.6 deliverable that consumes decomposition output\n99\t- `.claude/skills/try-n-approaches/SKILL.md` — S9 Karpathy autoresearch full\n100\t- D-003 § 5.5c.1 — strategic rationale + UP-06 §3 directive\n101\t- `references/classification-heuristics.md` — full decision rules\n102\t- `references/output-template.md` — structured output shape\n103\t"}]},"uuid":"782cc2d3-57bc-4fd2-a6eb-67dd3ede81e1","timestamp":"2026-04-29T13:07:30.806Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\.claude\\skills\\decompose-work\\SKILL.md","content":"---\nname: decompose-work\ndescription: Split a non-trivial task into deterministic-vs-LLM portions before execution. Use when planning multi-step work where some sub-tasks are mechanical (file ops, regex, count, schema validation) and others require judgment (synthesis, design, ambiguity resolution). Outputs structured plan that maps each portion to the cheapest competent tool. Pairs with capability-map.md (model × effort × task_class grounding) and try-n-approaches (S9 — Karpathy autoresearch loop).\nallowed-tools: [Read, Glob, Grep, Bash]\n---\n\n# Skill: decompose-work\n\n## Purpose\n\nKarpathy autoresearch loop foundation: BEFORE doing a task, **classify sub-parts as deterministic (cheap, cacheable, verifiable) vs LLM-required (judgment, ambiguous, generative)**. Mechanical work goes to scripts; LLM cycles go to where judgment is irreducible. Avoids the two failure modes: (a) burning LLM tokens on `wc -l` style work that bash does for free, (b) hand-coding logic that should be a one-paragraph judgment call.\n\nPer UP-06 §3 verbatim: *\"cần phải có một skill hoặc agent với khả năng chuyên biệt giúp nó 'bóc tách' được ra thế nào là 'deterministic' và thế nào là 'llm probability'\"*.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Task has ≥3 distinct sub-parts\n- Some sub-parts smell mechanical, others smell judgment-heavy\n- Estimated context cost ≥30K if done naively LLM-only\n- About to dispatch subagent — knowing the deterministic floor saves token budget\n- Authoring a new skill or hook — decomposition surfaces what belongs in the script vs prompt\n\n## When NOT to Use\n\n- Task is trivial (≤2 steps, single tool call)\n- Task is purely deterministic (just run the script directly)\n- Task is purely judgment-only with no measurable outputs (e.g., \"draft this paragraph\" — LLM end-to-end)\n- Already mid-execution and re-decomposing would just be ceremonious\n\n## Inputs\n\n| Arg | Required | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| `task_description` | yes | Free-form text describing the goal |\n| `--budget` | no | Approximate token budget (helps prioritize deterministic offload) |\n| `--depth` | no | `shallow` (3-5 portions) \\| `deep` (full sub-tree). Default shallow. |\n\n## Process\n\n1. **Parse task** — list candidate sub-parts. Quote verbatim phrases from `task_description`. Don't paraphrase yet — paraphrase loses signal.\n2. **Read `agent-workspace/memory/capability-map.md`** if exists — note which cells (model × effort × task_class) match this task's flavor. If map empty/missing, proceed without grounding (note in output).\n3. **Classify each sub-part** per `references/classification-heuristics.md`:\n   - **DETERMINISTIC** if: file-search/glob, line/byte/match count, regex extract, JSON/YAML parse, schema validate, format conversion, deterministic transformation, hash, sort, dedupe, binary diff\n   - **LLM-REQUIRED** if: synthesis across sources, design/naming, ambiguity arbitration, free-text generation, judgment under uncertainty, calibration interpretation, multi-perspective adversarial reasoning\n   - **HYBRID** if: deterministic skeleton + LLM interpretation step, OR LLM drives deterministic tool use, OR threshold-based escalation (e.g., \"auto-classify if confidence ≥0.8 else escalate to LLM\")\n4. **For each portion, propose the cheapest competent tool**:\n   - Deterministic → bash/grep/awk/node oneliner, hook script, or existing tool (Glob, Grep, Bash, Read)\n   - LLM → main session, subagent (specify which: opus/sonnet/haiku, fresh-ctx?), or skill\n   - Hybrid → deterministic gate + escalation path\n5. **Build integration plan** — sequence + data flow + escalation rules. Where does deterministic output feed LLM input? Where does LLM decision drive deterministic action? Where's the failure escape?\n6. **Surface risks** — what if classification is wrong? What if LLM portion exceeds budget? What's the fallback if deterministic step fails?\n7. **Output structured result** per `references/output-template.md`:\n   ```\n   ## Decomposition: <task name>\n   ### Deterministic portions (N)\n   - [D-1] sub-task → tool → expected output\n   ### LLM-required portions (N)\n   - [L-1] sub-task → agent/skill → why LLM needed\n   ### Hybrid portions (N)\n   - [H-1] sub-task → gate + escalation\n   ### Integration plan\n   1. ... 2. ... 3. ...\n   ### Risks + fallbacks\n   - ...\n   ### Capability-map grounding\n   - cells consulted: ... | gaps: ...\n   ```\n\n## Validation Pre-Conditions\n\n- `task_description` is non-empty + ≥1 sentence\n- Skill output has ≥1 deterministic portion (if everything LLM, the skill isn't earning its keep — recommend skipping)\n- Integration plan covers ALL portions (no orphan sub-tasks)\n\n## Anti-Patterns\n\n- **Treating \"code generation\" as fully deterministic** — design naming + interface choice is LLM. Only the resulting build/lint/test step is deterministic.\n- **Over-decomposing trivial tasks** — `cat foo` doesn't need this skill.\n- **Skipping integration plan** — listing parts without sequencing them is half a deliverable.\n- **Ignoring capability-map** — historical cells encode \"where LLM has hit limits\"; skipping them re-discovers those limits the hard way.\n- **Confusing \"LLM-required\" with \"complex\"** — a simple-feeling task with ambiguity (e.g., \"rename this thing well\") is LLM-required even if short.\n- **Decomposing AFTER execution** — that's a postmortem, not a decomposer; this skill is pre-execution.\n\n## Smoke Test (S8 first run)\n\nSample task: *\"Author Track 5.5c.6 promote-rule skill that scans agent-notes for clusterable rules and proposes promotion to hook/skill/charter.\"*\n\nExpected decomposition (target):\n- Deterministic: read agent-notes.md (Read), regex-extract rule headers (Grep), token-similarity heuristic (bash + node), output proposal markdown (Write)\n- LLM-required: cluster naming, promotion-target choice (hook vs skill vs charter), prevention-rule rephrasing\n- Hybrid: similarity threshold gate (deterministic) → if cluster ≥3, LLM evaluates whether promotion is warranted\n\nIf smoke test produces this rough shape, skill is functional.\n\n## See Also\n\n- `agent-workspace/memory/capability-map.md` — model × effort × task_class living grounding\n- `.claude/skills/promote-rule/SKILL.md` — sibling 5.5c.6 deliverable that consumes decomposition output\n- `.claude/skills/try-n-approaches/SKILL.md` — S9 Karpathy autoresearch full\n- D-003 § 5.5c.1 — strategic rationale + UP-06 §3 directive\n- `references/classification-heuristics.md` — full decision rules\n- `references/output-template.md` — structured output shape\n","numLines":103,"startLine":1,"totalLines":103}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"53025837-a92c-4dba-af41-661ebfc4ed03","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"45ff2d8f-d633-45f8-8a6e-c5fb5ce08842","version":"2.1.122","gitBranch":"main"}

My job: catch decay early, before it's systemic.\"\n\n## Responsibility\n\nExecute full drift signal scan + semantic checks that simple grep can't catch:\n- DR1-DR6: grep-based (also in /drift-check)\n- DR7: UL semantic check\n- DR8-DR10: cross-reference checks\n- DR11: staleness check\n- DR12: semantic anti-pattern detection (this requires LLM inspection)\n\n## Input\n\n- `agent-workspace/constitution/drift-signals.md` (definitions)\n- `agent-workspace/memory/agent-notes.md` (learned anti-patterns for DR12)\n- Access to codebase\n\n## Process\n\n### Phase 1: Run Automated Signals\n\nExecute grep/script signals from `drift-signals.md`:\n\n**DR1**: Domain layer framework imports\n```bash\ngrep -rn \"from fastapi\\|import fastapi\\|from pydantic\\|import pydantic\\|from sqlalchemy\\|import sqlalchemy\" \\\n  packages/domain/ --include=\"*.py\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"test_\\|_test\"\n```\n\n**DR2**: Evidence without citation (if DB accessible)\n```sql\nSELECT id, text FROM claims WHERE source_url IS NULL OR source_url = '';\n```\n\n**DR3**: LLM call without budget/retry\n```bash\ngrep -rn \"anthropic\\.Anthropic\\|client\\.messages\\.create\\|openai\\.OpenAI\" packages/infrastructure/ \\\n  --include=\"*.py\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"with_budget\\|with_retry\"\n```\n\n**DR4**: Large inline prompts outside prompts/\n```bash\n# Heuristic: long triple-quoted strings in Python files outside prompts/\ngrep -rEn '\"\"\"[^\"]{500,}\"\"\"' packages/application/ apps/ \\\n  --include=\"*.py\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"prompts/\"\n```\n\n**DR5**: Claim missing required metadata (SQL — if accessible)\n\n**DR6**: `Any` types in domain\n```bash\ngrep -rn \": Any\\|-> Any\\| Any,\" packages/domain/ \\\n  --include=\"*.py\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"test_\\|_test\"\n```\n\n**DR8**: Cross-BC direct imports\n```bash\nfor BC in market_data fundamental company_intelligence macro news influence crowd analysis portfolio; do\n  grep -rn \"from packages.domain\\.\" packages/domain/$BC/ \\\n    --include=\"*.py\" 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"packages.domain.$BC\\.\"\ndone\n```\n\n### Phase 2: Semantic Signals (LLM-inspected)\n\n**DR7**: UL term drift (semantic check)\n\nThis requires understanding synonyms, not just exact match:\n- Does code use \"Report\" where glossary expects \"Thesis\"?\n- Does code use \"Fact\" where glossary expects \"Claim\"?\n- Does code use \"Score\" where glossary expects \"CredibilityScore\"?\n- Any variable name suggesting concept not in glossary?\n\nScan for:\n1. Class names / type names\n2. Variable names in domain code\n3. Event names\n4. Method names\n\nFor each, compare against glossary semantically.\n\n**DR12**: Anti-patterns from agent-notes.md\n\nRead `agent-workspace/memory/agent-notes.md`.\nFor each documented anti-pattern, search code:\n- Pattern: [description]\n- Search strategy: [grep pattern or semantic signature]\n- Findings: [file:line with details]\n\n**DR10**: Spec cross-references\n```bash\n# Extract all spec IDs referenced\ngrep -rhoE \"SPEC-[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{3}\" . \\\n  --include=\"*.py\" --include=\"*.md\" 2>/dev/null | sort -u > /tmp/refs.txt\n\n# For each, check if file exists\nwhile read spec_id; do\n  found=$(find specs -name \"*${spec_id}*\" 2>/dev/null | head -1)\n  if [ -z \"$found\" ]; then\n    echo \"MISSING: $spec_id (referenced but not found)\"\n  fi\ndone < /tmp/refs.txt\n```\n\n**DR11**: Staleness\n```bash\n# Last modified of state files\nstat -c \"%Y %n\" agent-workspace/memory/project.md 2>/dev/null\nstat -c \"%Y %n\" agent-workspace/memory/current-execution.md 2>/dev/null\n\n# Compare with latest session\nLATEST_SESSION=$(ls -t agent-workspace/memory/sessions/ 2>/dev/null | head -1)\n# If project.md older than latest session, likely stale\n```\n\n### Phase 3: Synthesize Findings\n\nGroup by:\n- Severity (HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW)\n- Location (file)\n- Drift type (DR number)\n\n### Phase 4: Write Report\n\n```markdown\n# Drift Detector Report — YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM\n\n## Summary\n- Signals run: 12\n- HIGH violations: X\n- MEDIUM violations: Y\n- LOW violations: Z\n\n## Status\n[CLEAN | WARNINGS | HIGH VIOLATIONS BLOCK MERGE]\n\n## HIGH Severity (blocks commit/merge)\n\n### DR1: Domain framework imports (2 violations)\n\n1. `packages/domain/analysis/thesis.py:12`\n   ```python\n   from fastapi import HTTPException  # VIOLATION\n   ```\n   Fix: Move to interfaces layer. Keep domain pure.\n\n2. `packages/domain/fundamental/ratio.py:8`\n   ```python\n   from pydantic import BaseModel  # VIOLATION\n   ```\n   Fix: Use stdlib dataclasses in domain. Pydantic only in interfaces/infrastructure.\n\n### DR8: Cross-BC imports (1 violation)\n[...]\n\n## MEDIUM Severity (fix this session or next)\n\n### DR3: LLM calls without budget wrapper (3 systems, patterns, and abstractions.\n13\tWrites plans that a developer can execute without re-planning.\n14\t\n15\tMindset: \"A good plan gives the developer all decisions made — they just need to Part B makes execution unambiguous.\"\n\n## Responsibility\n\nGiven a feature name or brief, produce complete dual-layer spec following SPEC_TEMPLATE.md.\n\n## Input\n\n- Feature name or description\n- Relevant glossary sections\n- Related existing specs\n- Constitution constraints (architecture, invariants)\n\n## Process\n\n### Phase 1: Clarify Intent\n\nIf input is vague, ask clarifying questions:\n- Who is this for?\n- What problem does it solve?\n- What's out of scope?\n- What's success?\n\nDon't proceed until intent is clear.\n\n### Phase 2: Check Glossary\n\nFor every domain term used:\n- Does it exist in `agent-workspace/ubiquitous-language/glossary.md`?\n- Is usage consistent with definition?\n\nIf new terms emerge → flag for `/drill-me` session before finalizing spec.\n\n### Phase 3: Check Existing Specs\n\n- Is similar feature already spec'd?\n- Any related specs that inform this one?\n- Any deprecated specs that suggest what NOT to do?\n\n### Phase 4: Write Part A (Business)\n\nSections in order:\n- A.1 Context (narrative)\n- A.2 User & Use Cases (with acceptance scenarios)\n- A.3 Business Rules (in ubiquitous language)\n- A.4 Success Criteria (quantitative + qualitative)\n- A.5 Scope (in/out/deferred)\n- A.6 Key Decisions (options, chosen, tradeoffs)\n- A.7 Risks & Mitigations\n- A.8 Dependencies (upstream, downstream, external)\n- A.9 Glossary Check\n\n### Phase 5: Derive Part B (Agent Contract)\n\nFor each Part A section, derive technical specifics:\n- B.1 Input Contract (Python dataclass or Pydantic model per layer)\n- B.2 Output Contract (with guarantees)\n- B.3 Core Logic (pseudocode algorithm — no LLM math)\n- B.4 Data Model Changes\n- B.5 Affected Bounded Contexts\n- B.6 Domain Events\n- B.7 API Surface (if applicable — Phase 2+)\n- B.8 Non-Functional Requirements\n- B.9 Test Requirements (pyramid allocation + BDD)\n- B.10 Implementation Constraints (MUST/MUST NOT/SHOULD)\n- B.11 Dependencies on Skills/Subagents\n- B.12 Rollout Plan\n- B.13 Verification Checklist\n\n### Phase 6: Part C Initial\n\nAuthoring history entry.\nEmpty review slot.\n\n### Phase 7: Self-Review\n\nBefore finalizing:\n- Size check: Tier 2 should be 200-500 lines\n- Glossary consistency\n- Part A readable by non-engineer?\n- Part B unambiguous?\n- Risks realistic?\n- Stock-specific: No LLM math in B.3 logic? Source citation in output contract?\n\n### Phase 8: Write File\n\nSave to `specs/tier2-feature/NNN-<slug>.md` (next number available).\n\n### Phase 9: Report\n\nReturn to invoker:\n\n```markdown\n# Spec Created\n\nPath: [path]\nSpec ID: SPEC-YYYY-MM-DD-NNN\n\n## Part A Summary\n[2-3 sentences]\n\n## Key Decisions\n[D-1, D-2, D-3 summaries]\n\n## Open Questions\n[Things needing human input]\n\n## Glossary Status\n- Existing terms used: [list]\n- New terms: [list, recommend /drill-me]\n\n## Next Steps\n1. Review Part A with stakeholder\n2. Address open questions\n3. When approved: /spec-to-wiki\n4. Run /bdd-planner for test cases\n5. Run /master-plan for implementation\n```\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Use template structure (SPEC_TEMPLATE.md)\n- Part A narrative, Part B structured\n- Stay under 500 lines Tier 2\n- All terms in glossary or flagged\n- Derivation A→B must be clear\n- No LLM math in B.3 pseudocode (numbers must come from deterministic code)\n\n## Do NOT\n\n- Invent new terms silently (flag instead)\n- Make architectural decisions beyond spec (escalate)\n- Combine multiple features in one spec (split)\n- Skip Part C (provenance matters)\n- Write spec without clarifying unclear intent\n- Include a single \"buy/sell\" score in output contract (must be multi-criteria)\n\n## Related\n\n- Command: /spec-author\n- Template: SPEC_TEMPLATE.md\n- Skill: spec-dual-layer\n- Skill: ubiquitous-language\n","numLines":158,"startLine":1,"totalLines":158}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7c463f0c-3f81-451f-91f6-31632437494d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"45ff2d8f-d633-45f8-8a6e-c5fb5ce08842","version":"2.1.122","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"ce948078-7953-45ef-8084-540642748913","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01A2n1SbJMviEQJ6Dj7RQT9Z","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01S5DybeK5RJCvvTxq6L67B1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\.claude\\agents\\research-scanner.md","content":"---\nname: research-scanner\ndescription: Opensource repository surveyor with provenance. Reads README + recent commits of candidate repos and reports fit-for-stockforge with repo URL + commit SHA + as-of date for every claim. Use for agent-pick-1 dogfood (D-005 § 5.5d.3) and future opensource tool surveys.\nmodel: sonnet\ntools: [Read, Glob, Grep, WebFetch]\n---\n\n# Subagent: Research Scanner\n\n## Persona\n\nRepo cartographer. Surveys opensource ecosystems to find tools that materially advance stockforge's harness self-learning loop. Skeptical by default — most repos look exciting in README and disappoint in 10-minute integration. Trades novelty for fit.\n\nMindset: \"A tool earns adoption by closing a measurable loop in our system, not by being trendy. Provenance every claim.\"\n\n## Responsibility\n\nGiven a use case + candidate repo list, produce a ≤5-page structured report that:\n- Surveys each candidate via README + recent-commit signal\n- Scores fit on stockforge-specific axes (harness self-learning, Karpathy autoresearch, agent-notes/mistake-log integration, Phase-0 portability)\n- Picks ONE winner with explicit trade-off rationale\n- Provides repo URL + commit SHA + as-of date for every fact stated\n\nNever integrates a tool. Never installs dependencies. Read-only research artifact only.\n\n## Input\n\nFrom invoker:\n- Use case description (what loop is being closed)\n- Candidate list (≥2 repos; if \"agent-pick-1\", invoker may delegate enumeration)\n- Output path (typically `agent-workspace/learning-data/dogfood/<tool>.md` or sibling)\n- Selection criterion (verbatim, e.g., \"max alignment with stockforge harness self-learning\")\n\n## Process\n\n### Phase 1: Comprehend Use Case\n\nRead:\n- `agent-workspace/memory/decisions/005-up08-track-5.5d-self-learning-pipeline.md` § 5.5d.3 (dogfood criteria)\n- `agent-workspace/memory/capability-map.md` (limit rows L-1..L-12; especially L-1 NO-LLM-math)\n- `PROJECT_CHARTER.md` § \"Core Principles\" (charter constraints any tool must respect)\n- Any invoker-provided context\n\nRestate the use case in own words before scanning. If unclear, halt + ask.\n\n### Phase 2: Enumerate Candidates\n\nIf invoker provided list → use it verbatim.\nIf invoker said \"agent-pick-1\" with seed names (e.g., DSPy, LangSmith, openai/swarm, agno, llm.c-style autoresearch) → use seed list; do NOT broaden without explicit budget headroom.\n\nFor each candidate, capture:\n- Repo URL (canonical github.com/<owner>/<repo>)\n- Stated purpose (1 sentence from README header)\n- License (MIT/Apache/AGPL/proprietary — affects integration legality)\n- Last-commit indicator (from commits page; flags abandonware)\n\n### Phase 3: Per-Candidate Fact-Gathering (WebFetch)\n\nFor each candidate, fetch via WebFetch:\n1. `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/HEAD/README.md` (or `.rst`)\n2. `https://api.github.com/repos/<owner>/<repo>/commits?per_page=5` (recent commits — extract SHAs + dates)\n\nRecord verbatim:\n- README first 200 lines (or full if shorter)\n- 5 most recent commit SHAs + commit messages + dates\n\nDo NOT paraphrase commit messages or README. Store quotes inline. Provenance = exact byte-from-source.\n\n### Phase 4: Score on Stockforge Axes\n\nFor each candidate, evaluate (1-5 each):\n\n| Axis | Definition |\n|---|---|\n| **harness-self-learning fit** | Does it close the sessions/agent-notes/mistake-log → cap-map → promote-rule loop? |\n| **karpathy-autoresearch fit** | Does it support outer-loop framing (deepen/broaden/abandon decisions w/ measurement)? |\n| **agent-notes integration fit** | Can it consume markdown notes / NDJSON event streams stockforge already emits? |\n| **Phase-0 portability** | Runs on bash + node + POSIX (per L-S11-1)? Or requires Docker/postgres/redis (Phase-1+)? |\n| **provenance discipline** | Does the tool itself enforce source citation, or does it generate ungrounded text? |\n\nScore MUST cite source (README quote / commit message / docs URL). LLM may NOT compute scores from \"feel\" — every score has a quoted line.\n\n### Phase 5: Pick Winner — Trade-off Matrix\n\nOutput a multi-row matrix; identify the single highest-aligned candidate. Surface:\n- Why this beats runner-up (≥2 distinct points)\n- What this LOSES vs runner-up (honest trade-off)\n- ≥1 disqualifier surfaced for at least one rejected candidate (signal: scan was adversarial, not just confirming)\n\nIf two candidates tie within 1 point on aggregate → prefer the one with stronger Phase-0 portability (cheap-first ordering doctrine).\n\n### Phase 6: Adversarial Section\n\nMandatory bear-case section. ≥3 reasons the picked tool may be wrong choice 6 months from now:\n- Maintenance staleness (last commit > 90 days?)\n- License or governance risk (single-maintainer / contested fork?)\n- Integration cost vs alternative (could we build minimum equivalent in <1 session?)\n- Frontier-model substitution (does Claude 5/Opus 5 obsolete this in 6 months?)\n\nIf <3 distinct bear points emerge → either the tool is genuinely solid (rare) or scan is shallow (default assumption: scan was shallow; lengthen Phase 3-4).\n\n### Phase 7: Write Report\n\nSave to invoker-specified path. Format:\n\n```markdown\n---\nreport_id: research-scanner-<TS>\ncreated_at: <ISO-8601>\nuse_case: <verbatim from invoker>\ncandidates_scanned: <N>\npicked: <repo URL>\nas_of: <YYYY-MM-DD>\n---\n\n# Research-Scanner Report — <use case>\n\n## Summary\n- Picked: <owner/repo> @ <SHA-7> (<as_of date>)\n- Runner-up: <owner/repo>\n- Disqualified: <list with 1-line reason each>\n\n## Per-candidate findings (provenance-cited)\n[one section per candidate; Execute Phases 1-7 of that file.\n\n## Use case (verbatim from invoker)\n\nStockForge is a Phase-0 AI harness building a self-learning loop: telemetry events (NDJSON) → sessions/agent-notes/mistake-log corpus → capability-map (model × effort × task_class limits) → promote-rule (cluster recurring rules to hooks/skills/charter) → Karpathy outer-loop framing (deepen/broaden/abandon experiments). I need to pick ONE opensource repo whose ideas/patterns most closely advance THIS specific loop. We are NOT picking a stock-data tool; we are picking a self-learning / autoresearch / agent-evaluation pattern source.\n\n## Candidate seed list (do NOT broaden)\n\n1. `stanfordnlp/dspy` — declarative LLM pipeline + prompt optimizers\n2. `langchain-ai/langsmith-sdk` — observability + eval tooling\n3. `openai/swarm` — minimal multi-agent orchestration\n4. `agno-agi/agno` — agent framework with memory + reasoning patterns\n5. `karpathy/llm.c` — pedagogical reference (autoresearch loop spirit; not infra)\n6. `langchain-ai/openevals` — eval framework\n\n## Selection criterion (verbatim)\n\n\"Max alignment with StockForge's harness self-learning loop: sessions/agent-notes/mistake-log → cap-map → promote-rule → Karpathy framing. Prefer Phase-0 portable (bash+node+POSIX; no Docker/Postgres/Redis required to extract value). {"parentUuid":"fdd338ee-3b80-4804-9917-bba1ec174e76","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"fc11436c-fada-41bc-8ae7-32ac3f984211","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CtCjFYMiCUwpmBVEeEx3WP","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Agent Notes — Learned Rules\n2\t\n3\t> Append-mostly. Each rule earned through real experience (post-mortem, drift detection, user correction).\n4\t> Rules here have higher status than convention but lower than constitution.\n5\t\n6\t## Rule Format\n7\t\n8\tUse this template:\n9\t\n10\t```markdown\n11\t### YYYY-MM-DD: [Short rule name]\n12\t**Context**: What situation triggered learning this\n13\t**Rule**: The actionable rule\n14\t**Anti-example**: What was done wrong\n15\t**Correct example**: What should be done instead\n16\t**Severity**: critical | high | medium | low\n17\t**Auto-detect**: yes/no — can a drift signal catch this?\n18\t```\n19\t\n20\t---\n21\t\n22\t## Initial Rules (from charter design)\n23\t\n24\t### 2026-04-23: LLM Never Outputs Numbers\n25\t**Context**: Stock domain risk — LLM-generated numbers in finance lead to real money loss\n26\t**Rule**: LLM never returns numerical values as natural-language output. All numbers come from deterministic Python functions invoked via tool use. LLM only interprets results.\n27\t**Anti-example**:\n28\t```\n29\tLLM output: \"Based on my analysis, the company's ROE is approximately 18%, P/E is around 12, and intrinsic value is roughly 95,000 VND per share.\"\n30\t```\n31\t**Correct example**:\n32\t```\n33\tLLM tool calls:\n34\t  compute_roe(net_income=2_345B, equity=13_120B) → 17.87%\n35\t  compute_pe(price=85_000, eps=7_054) → 12.05\n36\t  compute_intrinsic_value_dcf(...) → 94_523 VND\n37\tLLM output: \"ROE = 17.87% (FY2024), P/E = 12.05 (TTM), DCF intrinsic value = 94,523 VND. Versus current price 85,000 VND, suggests ~11% margin of safety.\"\n38\t```\n39\t**Severity**: critical\n40\t**Auto-detect**: yes — regex for \"approximately\", \"around\", \"roughly\" + numbers in LLM output\n41\t\n42\t### 2026-04-23: Bear Case Required for Every Thesis\n43\t**Context**: Confirmation bias is the #1 retail investor mistake\n44\t**Rule**: Every thesis output must include substantive bear case (≥3 distinct points, each grounded in evidence). System refuses to render thesis without it.\n45\t**Anti-example**: Thesis with bull case + risks section that says \"market volatility, regulatory changes\" (boilerplate)\n46\t**Correct example**: Bear case with specific points like \"Q3 inventory turnover dropped 23% YoY suggesting demand weakness\", \"Major competitor X launched directly competitive product Y in March 2026\", \"Net working capital deterioration of 15% indicates cash flow strain\"\n47\t**Severity**: high\n48\t**Auto-detect**: yes — bear case length + specificity check\n49\t\n50\t### 2026-04-23: Confidence Requires Calibration Data\n51\t**Context**: LLM \"confidence\" is qualitative; user reads numerical values\n52\t**Rule**: Any \"X% confidence\" or \"high/medium/low confidence\" claim must trace to historical hit rate from calibration database. Include `n_samples`, `lookback_period`, `hit_rate` metadata.\n53\t**Anti-example**: \"70% confidence this is a buy\"\n54\t**Correct example**: \"Confidence: MEDIUM (signal pattern: cyclical_bottom_with_dividend; historical n=23 cases since 2018; hit rate 65% over 12 months; lookback=78 months)\"\n55\t**Severity**: high\n56\t**Auto-detect**: partial — flag confidence claims without metadata\n57\t\n58\t### 2026-04-23: Source Provider Disagreement Surfaced\n59\t**Context**: vnstock vs FiinPro vs TCBS sometimes disagree on same data point\n60\t**Rule**: When sources disagree by >1%, output shows divergence explicitly. Never silently pick one.\n61\t**Anti-example**: \"Revenue Q3 2024: 35,000 tỷ\" (silent average or arbitrary pick)\n62\t**Correct example**: \"Revenue Q3 2024: 35,000 tỷ ± 200 tỷ (vnstock: 35,000 / FiinPro: 35,200 / TCBS: 34,800; reconciled with HIGH confidence)\"\n63\t**Severity**: medium\n64\t**Auto-detect**: no (requires source metadata in output template)\n65\t\n66\t---\n67\t\n68\t## Add New Rules Here\n69\t\n70\t### 2026-04-29: Q&A Bundle Skeleton MUST Carry `defer_cycle` Field\n71\t**Context**: While drafting `qa-escalation/references/sample-bundle.md` in S2, I initially omitted `defer_cycle` from the bundle frontmatter — caught on review against Decision 002 § Track 2 R7 mitigation.\n72\t**Rule**: Every Q&A bundle file in `human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/` MUST include `defer_cycle: 0` at creation, and increment on every reopening / supersession. SessionStart hook (Track 5) keys can-kicking detection off this field.\n73\t**Anti-example**: Bundle with frontmatter `id / topic / opened_at / expected_answer_by / priority / status` — passes superficial validation but R7 mitigation is invisible.\n74\t**Correct example**: Bundle frontmatter includes `defer_cycle: 0` from creation; every Decision in `related_decisions:` also has `defer_cycles` field syncronized.\n75\t**Severity**: medium\n76\t**Auto-detect**: yes — Track 5 `qa-pending-stale-mover.sh` hook should reject bundles missing the field.\n77\t\n78\t### 2026-04-29: User Communication Idiom — \"ok rồi. continue\" / \"ok continue\" = Trivial Acknowledgment\n79\t**Context**: User in `human-workspace/user_prompt/20260429_03.txt` and via chat replies repeatedly used the idiom \"ok rồi. continue\" or \"ok continue\" as the standard \"I accept the proposed defaults; proceed\" signal in Q&A round acceptance flows.\n80\t**Rule**: Treat both phrases as TRIVIAL acknowledgment in lite-detect (`user-prompt-intake/references/lite-detect-patterns.md`). Do NOT dispatch intent-classifier on these. They specifically mean \"accept all defaults of the most recent agent-proposed bundle / decision\".\n81\t**Anti-example**: Spending 5K context to classify \"ok continue\" via subagent — wasteful and misses the user's stable communication idiom.\n82\t**Correct example**: Lite-detect short-circuits these to `primary_intent: TRIVIAL` + `recommended_action: HANDLE_TRIVIAL`. Continue per `current-execution.md`.\n83\t**Severity**: low (efficiency, not correctness)\n84\t**Auto-detect**: yes — already covered in lite-detect whitelist as \"special idiom\" entry.\n85\t\n86\t### 2026-04-29: Hooks Invoked by Harness Shell Don't Traverse Claude Permission Model\n87\t**Context**: While designing Q&A escalation in S2, I encountered a tension: `Write(human-workspace/q-and-a/answered/**)` is deny-listed for the agent (only humans move files there), but Track 5 hooks may need to write `status: processed` flag inside answered files.\n88\t**Rule**: Hooks invoked by Claude Code harness (configured in `.claude/settings.json` `hooks` section) execute as shell scripts OUTSIDE the agent's tool-permission model. The deny-list applies to the agent's Write/Edit tool calls, not to hook scripts. Therefore hook scripts CAN write to deny-listed paths, but should still respect the spirit (don't pretend to be human-authored content).\n89\t**Anti-example**: Refusing to write a Track 5 hook that updates `status: processed` because \"the agent can't write to answered/\".\n90\t**Correct example**: Hook script writes the `status: processed` flag; agent's tool calls remain blocked. If unsure, prefer sidecar files (`<bundle>.processed`) over in-place edits to keep audit trail crisp.\n91\t**Severity**: medium\n92\t**Auto-detect**: no (design-time consideration)\n93\t\n94\t### 2026-04-29 (post-audit): AP-S2-3 — False Self-Attestation on Measurable Properties\n95\t**Context**: In S2 session log \"verifier-lite pass\" I claimed: \"SKILL.md bodies are 130-160 LOC each…within target\". Adversarial drift audit (background `general-purpose` subagent) measured actual LOC and found `qa-escalation` = 242 (+61% over) and `grill-maximization` = 187 (+25% over). My self-attestation was wrong by ~25-61%, while feeling confident.\n96\t**Rule**: For any property that can be measured deterministically (LOC, byte count, presence-of-field, file-exists), VERIFY with the actual command (`wc -l`, `Glob`, `Grep`) before writing the verifier-lite claim. Do NOT estimate from memory of \"what I just wrote feels about right.\"\n97\t**Anti-example**: \"manual count: SKILL.md bodies are 130-160 LOC each…within target\" written without running `wc -l`.\n98\t**Correct example**: Run `wc -l <file>` for each verified item; quote the actual number. If above ceiling, either trim or document the overrun + grace flag explicitly.\n99\t**Severity**: high (this is the same anti-pattern as orch's CF-DOGFOOD-2 self-attestation — agent claims compliance based on impression, not verification)\n100\t**Auto-detect**: yes — Track 5 hook can grep session logs for `LOC|line count|bodies are.*LOC` claims and require accompanying `wc -l` command in the same session log."}]},"uuid":"28e10173-add3-4505-bde7-587416f2f7d7","timestamp":"2026-04-29T13:10:54.011Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\agent-workspace\\memory\\agent-notes.md","content":"# Agent Notes — Learned Rules\n\n> Append-mostly. Each rule earned through real experience (post-mortem, drift detection, user correction).\n> Rules here have higher status than convention but lower than constitution.\n\n## Rule Format\n\nUse this template:\n\n```markdown\n### YYYY-MM-DD: [Short rule name]\n**Context**: What situation triggered learning this\n**Rule**: The actionable rule\n**Anti-example**: What was done wrong\n**Correct example**: What should be done instead\n**Severity**: critical | high | medium | low\n**Auto-detect**: yes/no — can a drift signal catch this?\n```\n\n---\n\n## Initial Rules (from charter design)\n\n### 2026-04-23: LLM Never Outputs Numbers\n**Context**: Stock domain risk — LLM-generated numbers in finance lead to real money loss\n**Rule**: LLM never returns numerical values as natural-language output. All numbers come from deterministic Python functions invoked via tool use. LLM only interprets results.\n**Anti-example**:\n```\nLLM output: \"Based on my analysis, the company's ROE is approximately 18%, P/E is around 12, and intrinsic value is roughly 95,000 VND per share.\"\n```\n**Correct example**:\n```\nLLM tool calls:\n  compute_roe(net_income=2_345B, equity=13_120B) → 17.87%\n  compute_pe(price=85_000, eps=7_054) → 12.05\n  compute_intrinsic_value_dcf(...) → 94_523 VND\nLLM output: \"ROE = 17.87% (FY2024), P/E = 12.05 (TTM), DCF intrinsic value = 94,523 VND. Versus current price 85,000 VND, suggests ~11% margin of safety.\"\n```\n**Severity**: critical\n**Auto-detect**: yes — regex for \"approximately\", \"around\", \"roughly\" + numbers in LLM output\n\n### 2026-04-23: Bear Case Required for Every Thesis\n**Context**: Confirmation bias is the #1 retail investor mistake\n**Rule**: Every thesis output must include substantive bear case (≥3 distinct points, each grounded in evidence). System refuses to render thesis without it.\n**Anti-example**: Thesis with bull case + risks section that says \"market volatility, regulatory changes\" (boilerplate)\n**Correct example**: Bear case with specific points like \"Q3 inventory turnover dropped 23% YoY suggesting demand weakness\", \"Major competitor X launched directly competitive product Y in March 2026\", \"Net working capital deterioration of 15% indicates cash flow strain\"\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: yes — bear case length + specificity check\n\n### 2026-04-23: Confidence Requires Calibration Data\n**Context**: LLM \"confidence\" is qualitative; user reads numerical values\n**Rule**: Any \"X% confidence\" or \"high/medium/low confidence\" claim must trace to historical hit rate from calibration database. Include `n_samples`, `lookback_period`, `hit_rate` metadata.\n**Anti-example**: \"70% confidence this is a buy\"\n**Correct example**: \"Confidence: MEDIUM (signal pattern: cyclical_bottom_with_dividend; historical n=23 cases since 2018; hit rate 65% over 12 months; lookback=78 months)\"\n**Severity**: high\n**Auto-detect**: partial — flag confidence claims without metadata\n\n### 2026-04-23: Source Provider Disagreement Surfaced\n**Context**: vnstock vs FiinPro vs TCBS sometimes disagree on same data point\n**Rule**: When sources disagree by >1%, output shows divergence explicitly. Never silently pick one.\n**Anti-example**: \"Revenue Q3 2024: 35,000 tỷ\" (silent average or arbitrary pick)\n**Correct example**: \"Revenue Q3 2024: 35,000 tỷ ± 200 tỷ (vnstock: 35,000 / FiinPro: 35,200 / TCBS: 34,800; reconciled with HIGH confidence)\"\n**Severity**: medium\n**Auto-detect**: no (requires source metadata in output template)\n\n---\n\n## Add New Rules Here\n\n### 2026-04-29: Q&A Bundle Skeleton MUST Carry `defer_cycle` Field\n**Context**: While drafting `qa-escalation/references/sample-bundle.md` in S2, I initially omitted `defer_cycle` from the bundle frontmatter — caught on review against Decision 002 § Track 2 R7 mitigation.\n**Rule**: Every Q&A bundle file in `human-workspace/q-and-a/pending/` MUST include `defer_cycle: 0` at creation, and increment on every reopening / supersession. SessionStart hook (Track 5) keys can-kicking detection off this field.\n**Anti-example**: Bundle with frontmatter `id / topic / opened_at / expected_answer_by / priority / status` — passes superficial validation but R7 mitigation is invisible.\n**Correct example**: Bundle frontmatter includes `defer_cycle: 0` from creation; every Decision in `related_decisions:` also has `defer_cycles` field syncronized.\n**Severity**: medium\n**Auto-detect**: yes — Track 5 `qa-pending-stale-mover.sh` hook should reject bundles missing the field.\n\n### 2026-04-29: User Communication Idiom — \"ok rồi. continue\" / \"ok continue\" = Trivial Acknowledgment\n**Context**: User in `human-workspace/user_prompt/20260429_03.txt` and via chat replies repeatedly used the idiom \"ok rồi. continue\" or \"ok continue\" as the standard \"I accept the proposed defaults; proceed\" signal in Q&A round acceptance flows.\n**Rule**: Treat both phrases as TRIVIAL acknowledgment in lite-detect (`user-prompt-intake/references/lite-detect-patterns.md`). Do NOT dispatch intent-classifier on these. They specifically mean \"accept all defaults of the most recent agent-proposed bundle / decision\".\n**Anti-example**: Spending 5K context to classify \"ok continue\" via subagent — wasteful and misses the user's stable communication idiom.\n**Correct example**: Lite-detect short-circuits these to `primary_intent: TRIVIAL` + `recommended_action: HANDLE_TRIVIAL`. Continue per `current-execution.md`.\n**Severity**: low (efficiency, not correctness)\n**Auto-detect**: yes — already covered in lite-detect whitelist as \"special idiom\" entry.\n\n### 2026-04-29: Hooks Invoked by Harness Shell Don't Traverse Claude Permission Model\n**Context**: While designing Q&A escalation in S2, I encountered a tension: `Write(human-workspace/q-and-a/answered/**)` is deny-listed for the agent (only humans move files there), but Track 5 hooks may need to write `status: processed` flag inside answered files.\n**Rule**: Hooks invoked by Claude Code harness (configured in `.claude/settings.json` `hooks` section) execute as shell scripts OUTSIDE the agent's tool-permission model. The deny-list applies to the agent's Write/Edit tool calls, not to hook scripts. Therefore hook scripts CAN write to deny-listed paths, but should still respect the spirit (don't pretend to be human-authored content).\n**Anti-example**: Refusing to write a Track 5 hook that updates `status: processed` because \"the agent can't write to answered/\".\n**Correct example**: Hook script writes the `status: processed` flag; agent's tool calls remain blocked. If unsure, prefer sidecar files (`<bundle>.processed`) over in-place edits to keep audit trail crisp.\n**Severity**: medium\n**Auto-detect**: no (design-time consideration)\n\n### 2026-04-29 (post-audit): AP-S2-3 — False Self-Attestation on Measurable Properties\n**Context**: In S2 session log \"verifier-lite pass\" I claimed: \"SKILL.md bodies are 130-160 LOC each…within target\". Adversarial drift audit (background `general-purpose` subagent) measured actual LOC and found `qa-escalation` = 242 (+61% over) and `grill-maximization` = 187 (+25% over). My self-attestation was wrong by ~25-61%, while feeling confident.\n**Rule**: For any property that can be measured deterministically (LOC, byte count, presence-of-field, file-exists), VERIFY with the actual command (`wc -l`, `Glob`, `Grep`) before writing the verifier-lite claim. Do NOT estimate from memory of \"what I just wrote feels about right.\"\n**Anti-example**: \"manual count: SKILL.md bodies are 130-160 LOC each…within target\" written without running `wc -l`.\n**Correct example**: Run `wc -l <file>` for each verified item; quote the actual number. If above ceiling, either trim or document the overrun + grace flag explicitly.\n**Severity**: high (this is the same anti-pattern as orch's CF-DOGFOOD-2 self-attestation — agent claims compliance based on impression, not verification)\n**Auto-detect**: yes — Track 5 hook can grep session logs for `LOC|line count|bodies are.*LOC` claims and require accompanying `wc -l` command in the same session log.","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":227}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fdd338ee-3b80-4804-9917-bba1ec174e76","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"45ff2d8f-d633-45f8-8a6e-c5fb5ce08842","version":"2.1.122","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"0e69348a-2e6e-42ef-a468-f01bd78f15cd","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"fc11436c-fada-41bc-8ae7-32ac3f984211","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HWkHuBAyUanddvqyXSf7i9","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# learning-data/ — Boundary Discipline (Track 5.5d.1)\n2\t\n3\t> **Status**: write-heavy data ETL store. **Runtime read path MUST NOT load this tree** (drift signal D9 enforces).\n4\t> **Established**: D-005 § 5.5d.1 (UP-08 ratification, 2026-04-29).\n5\t\n6\t## Why this directory exists\n7\t\n8\tPer UP-08 (Vietnamese verbatim): *\"self-learning/upgrade lại nặng về bài toán 'write', 'index', 'cache', nơi mà thu thập càng nhiều dữ liệu... với chi phí rẻ nhất mà không làm ảnh hưởng tới runtime\"*.\n9\t\n10\tUser-provided framing: **harness engineering = backend engineering / data ETL**. Runtime is read-heavy + reasoning + delegating; self-learning is write-heavy + indexing + caching. They are different disciplines. Conflating them in shared storage causes runtime to load write-only data accidentally (size penalty + relevance dilution).\n11\t\n12\tThis tree isolates the write-heavy stream from the read-heavy memory layer (`agent-workspace/memory/`) so that:\n13\t- Runtime context loads stay small and relevant\n14\t- Background analysis can scan a large event corpus without polluting per-session context\n15\t- Boundary violations are detectable via deterministic drift signal (D9), not vibes\n16\t\n17\t## Subdirectories\n18\t\n19\t| Path | Purpose | Tracked? | Read by runtime? |\n20\t|---|---|---|---|\n21\t| `events/` | Append-only NDJSON event stream emitted by hooks (Track 5.5d.1 D-2). One file per UTC date: `YYYY-MM-DD.ndjson`. | **gitignored** (volume + ephemerality) | **NO** — D9 violation if loaded |\n22\t| `archive/` | Rotated events older than 7 days (Track 5.5d.2 sweeper, S11). Purged at >30 days. | **gitignored** | **NO** — D9 violation if loaded |\n23\t| `index/` | RAG query target (Track 5.5d.2 index rebuild, S11). Phase 0: SQLite FTS5. Phase 1+: pgvector. | tracked (small; useful for diff review) | **YES** — read-allowed (RAG query path) |\n24\t| `dogfood/` | Opensource-tool integration insight artifacts (Track 5.5d.3, S12). One file per tool studied. | tracked | **YES** — read-allowed (insight artifacts are reference material) |\n25\t| `loop/` | Karpathy outer-loop experiment-framing artifacts (Track 5.5d.3, S12). One file per loop iteration. | tracked | **YES** — read-allowed |\n26\t\n27\t## Boundary contract (BINDING)\n28\t\n29\t1. **Write-only via hooks for `events/` + `archive/`.** Runtime sessions never write to these; only hook scripts (`component-telemetry.sh`, `learning-queue-sweeper.sh`, etc.) emit/rotate.\n30\t2. **Runtime read deny for `events/` + `archive/`.** Enforced by `.claude/settings.json` permissions (deny Read) + drift signal D9 (greps runtime code paths for `learning-data/events/` or `learning-data/archive/` references).\n31\t3. **`index/` is the runtime query surface.** When sessions need to query learning data, they read pre-built index files, not raw events. Phase 0 = SQLite FTS5; Phase 1+ = pgvector.\n32\t4. **`dogfood/` + `loop/` are append-only.** Sessions append; never delete or rewrite. History is the artifact.\n33\t5. **Schema**: NDJSON events MUST include `ts` (ISO-8601 UTC), `source` (which hook emitted), `event_type`, `payload`, `as_of` (per I-S2 — provenance). Set at S10 (D-2 deliverable).\n34\t\n35\t## Anti-patterns\n36\t\n37\t- **AP-1**: Loading `learning-data/events/*.ndjson` into a runtime session prompt. Fix: query `index/` instead.\n38\t- **AP-2**: Emitting events from non-hook code (e.g., session-end manual write). Fix: route through `component-telemetry.sh` hook to keep emission deterministic.\n39\t- **AP-3**: Deleting historical archives \"to save space\". Fix: sweeper handles rotation deterministically; manual delete = lost calibration data.\n40\t\n41\t## Connection to existing harness\n42\t\n43\t- Extends `scripts/hooks/component-telemetry.sh` (5.5c.5 JSONL telemetry pattern shipped S8) — adds learning-event emission alongside existing component-telemetry stream.\n44\t- Extended by `scripts/hooks/drift-signals-D1-D9.sh` (renamed from D1-D8 in S10) — D9 detects boundary violations.\n45\t- Read by future `scripts/hooks/learning-queue-sweeper.sh` (5.5d.2 S11) + `scripts/hooks/learning-index-rebuild.sh` (5.5d.2 S11).\n46\t- Consumed by `.claude/agents/research-scanner.md` (5.5d.3 S12) for opensource-tool dogfood.\n47\t\n48\t## References\n49\t\n50\t- Decision: `agent-workspace/memory/decisions/005-up08-track-5.5d-self-learning-pipeline.md`\n51\t- Decomposition: `agent-workspace/memory/observations/decompose-work-up08-S9.md`\n52\t- User prompt: `human-workspace/user_prompt/20260429_08.txt`\n53\t- Plan: `agent-workspace/session-plans/pending/002-track-5.5-sync-layer-selfcap.md` § 5.5d\n54\t"}]},"uuid":"fa48e9d5-557c-4317-b1b7-9f9cc24f76ba","timestamp":"2026-04-29T13:10:54.453Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\agent-workspace\\learning-data\\README.md","content":"# learning-data/ — Boundary Discipline (Track 5.5d.1)\n\n> **Status**: write-heavy data ETL store. **Runtime read path MUST NOT load this tree** (drift signal D9 enforces).\n> **Established**: D-005 § 5.5d.1 (UP-08 ratification, 2026-04-29).\n\n## Why this directory exists\n\nPer UP-08 (Vietnamese verbatim): *\"self-learning/upgrade lại nặng về bài toán 'write', 'index', 'cache', nơi mà thu thập càng nhiều dữ liệu... với chi phí rẻ nhất mà không làm ảnh hưởng tới runtime\"*.\n\nUser-provided framing: **harness engineering = backend engineering / data ETL**. Runtime is read-heavy + reasoning + delegating; self-learning is write-heavy + indexing + caching. They are different disciplines. Conflating them in shared storage causes runtime to load write-only data accidentally (size penalty + relevance dilution).\n\nThis tree isolates the write-heavy stream from the read-heavy memory layer (`agent-workspace/memory/`) so that:\n- Runtime context loads stay small and relevant\n- Background analysis can scan a large event corpus without polluting per-session context\n- Boundary violations are detectable via deterministic drift signal (D9), not vibes\n\n## Subdirectories\n\n| Path | Purpose | Tracked? | Read by runtime? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `events/` | Append-only NDJSON event stream emitted by hooks (Track 5.5d.1 D-2). One file per UTC date: `YYYY-MM-DD.ndjson`. | **gitignored** (volume + ephemerality) | **NO** — D9 violation if loaded |\n| `archive/` | Rotated events older than 7 days (Track 5.5d.2 sweeper, S11). Purged at >30 days. | **gitignored** | **NO** — D9 violation if loaded |\n| `index/` | RAG query target (Track 5.5d.2 index rebuild, S11). Phase 0: SQLite FTS5. Phase 1+: pgvector. | tracked (small; useful for diff review) | **YES** — read-allowed (RAG query path) |\n| `dogfood/` | Opensource-tool integration insight artifacts (Track 5.5d.3, S12). One file per tool studied. | tracked | **YES** — read-allowed (insight artifacts are reference material) |\n| `loop/` | Karpathy outer-loop experiment-framing artifacts (Track 5.5d.3, S12). One file per loop iteration. | tracked | **YES** — read-allowed |\n\n## Boundary contract (BINDING)\n\n1. **Write-only via hooks for `events/` + `archive/`.** Runtime sessions never write to these; only hook scripts (`component-telemetry.sh`, `learning-queue-sweeper.sh`, etc.) emit/rotate.\n2. **Runtime read deny for `events/` + `archive/`.** Enforced by `.claude/settings.json` permissions (deny Read) + drift signal D9 (greps runtime code paths for `learning-data/events/` or `learning-data/archive/` references).\n3. **`index/` is the runtime query surface.** When sessions need to query learning data, they read pre-built index files, not raw events. Phase 0 = SQLite FTS5; Phase 1+ = pgvector.\n4. **`dogfood/` + `loop/` are append-only.** Sessions append; never delete or rewrite. History is the artifact.\n5. **Schema**: NDJSON events MUST include `ts` (ISO-8601 UTC), `source` (which hook emitted), `event_type`, `payload`, `as_of` (per I-S2 — provenance). Set at S10 (D-2 deliverable).\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n- **AP-1**: Loading `learning-data/events/*.ndjson` into a runtime session prompt. Fix: query `index/` instead.\n- **AP-2**: Emitting events from non-hook code (e.g., session-end manual write). Fix: sweeper handles rotation deterministically; manual delete = lost calibration data.\n\n## Connection to existing harness\n\n- Extends `scripts/hooks/component-telemetry.sh` (5.5c.5 JSONL telemetry pattern shipped S8) — adds learning-event emission alongside existing component-telemetry stream.\n- Extended by `scripts/hooks/drift-signals-D1-D9.sh` (renamed from D1-D8 in S10) — D9 detects boundary violations.\n- Read by future `scripts/hooks/learning-queue-sweeper.sh` (5.5d.2 S11) + `scripts/hooks/learning-index-rebuild.sh` (5.5d.2 S11).\n- Consumed by `.claude/agents/research-scanner.md` (5.5d.3 S12) for opensource-tool dogfood.\n\n## References\n\n- Decision: `agent-workspace/memory/decisions/005-up08-track-5.5d-self-learning-pipeline.md`\n- Decomposition: `agent-workspace/memory/observations/decompose-work-up08-S9.md`\n- User prompt: `human-workspace/user_prompt/20260429_08.txt`\n- Plan: `agent-workspace/session-plans/pending/002-track-5.5-sync-layer-selfcap.md` § 5.5d\n","numLines":54,"startLine":1,"totalLines":54}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0e69348a-2e6e-42ef-a468-f01bd78f15cd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"45ff2d8f-d633-45f8-8a6e-c5fb5ce08842","version":"2.1.122","gitBranch":"main"}

Pick ONE opensource repo whose ideas/patterns most closely advance THIS specific loop. NOT a stock-data tool; a self-learning / autoresearch / agent-evaluation pattern source.\"\n5\tcandidates_scanned: 6\n6\tpicked: https://github.com/stanfordnlp/dspy\n7\tas_of: 2026-04-29\n8\t---\n9\t\n10\t# Research-Scanner Report — StockForge Harness Self-Learning Loop (agent-pick-1)\n11\t\n12\t## Phase 1 — Use Case Restatement (own words)\n13\t\n14\tStockForge's harness emits structured telemetry (NDJSON events) from every agent session. These events\n15\taccumulate in `learning-data/events/`. A background sweep periodically indexes them (SQLite FTS5 Phase 0)\n16\tand runs a classification pass to identify drift clusters, mistake types, and promotion candidates.\n17\tThe outputs update `capability-map.md` (model × effort × task_class grounding) and surface rules for\n18\t`promote-rule` to push into deterministic hooks/skills/charter. The Karpathy outer loop then frames the\n19\tnext experiment (deepen/broaden/abandon) using measurement from prior cycles.\n20\t\n21\t**Selection criterion**: the best repo closes this loop at pattern level — it provides ideas/structures\n22\tfor (a) evaluation of agent behavior, (b) self-improving signal collection, (c) outer-loop framing\n23\twith measurable deepen/broaden/abandon decisions. Phase-0 portability (bash + POSIX; no Docker/Postgres\n24\trequired to extract value from the patterns) is the tiebreaker. Charter principles 1 (evidence grounding),\n25\t8 (calibration over confidence), 9 (no LLM math) must be respected by the candidate's own design.\n26\t\n27\t---\n28\t\n29\t## Summary\n30\t\n31\t- **Picked**: `stanfordnlp/dspy` @ `db83e5a` (2026-04-28)\n32\t- **Runner-up**: `langchain-ai/openevals`\n33\t- **Disqualified**:\n34\t  - `openai/swarm` — explicitly superseded by OpenAI Agents SDK; Claude Sonnet 4.6 / Opus 4.7 at high effort already follow structured\n261\tinstructions reliably (capability-map Strength row #1: \"Multi-mechanism ensemble design lands well\").\n262\tAs frontier models improve at instruction following, the marginal value of DSPy-style prompt optimization\n263\tshrinks. If Claude 5 / Sonnet 5 natively generalizes from minimal examples, the \"compile\" step becomes\n264\tredundant. Risk window: 6-12 months.\n265\t\n266\t**Bear point 2 — Abstraction mismatch with StockForge's bash-first harness**\n267\t\n268\tDSPy is a Python framework designed to wrap LLM calls in Python module objects (`dspy.Module`,\n269\t`dspy.Predict`). StockForge's Phase-0 harness is bash + POSIX hooks + NDJSON files — there is no\n270\tPython LLM call layer yet (Phase 0 operates at the Claude Code CLI level, not API level). Extracting\n271\tDSPy patterns for a bash harness requires a translation step that is non-trivial. The \"dogfood\"\n272\tvalue in 5.5d.3 may be pattern-level only (reading optimizer source code) rather than running DSPy\n273\tagainst real StockForge data. This reduces the dogfood signal quality for the 5.5d.3 success criterion\n274\t(\"≥1 measurable insight\").\n275\t\n276\t**Bear point 3 — Maintenance governance risk**\n277\t\n278\tDSPy is a Stanford research project (Stanford Future Data Systems lab). Research group repos have\n279\tnon-commercial governance — direction is driven by PhD students and papers, not by production user\n280\tfeedback. If the core research team graduates and moves\n282\ton, or if DSPy is absorbed into a larger framework (LangGraph has already built similar optimization\n283\tprimitives), maintenance could stall. Last commit is 1 day old (HEALTHY now), but governance\n284\tconcentration in a university research context is a structural fragility.\n285\t\n286\t**Bear point 4 (bonus) — LLM-as-judge risk in optimizer**\n287\t\n288\tSome DSPy optimizers (notably MIPRO variants) use an LLM to generate candidate instructions as part\n289\tof the optimization loop. If StockForge adopted DSPy's full optimizer stack (not just its patterns),\n290\tthe LLM-generated instruction candidates could introduce hallucinated optimization steps — which\n291\tconflicts with charter principle 9 (no LLM math) if the LLM-generated instructions affect numeric\n292\treasoning paths. Mitigation: StockForge should adopt DSPy's metric-function pattern and compilation\n293\tframing, not the full MIPRO optimizer, for the 5.5d.3 dogfood. These events\naccumulate in `learning-data/events/`. A background sweep periodically indexes them (SQLite FTS5 Phase 0)\nand runs a classification pass to identify drift clusters, mistake types, and promotion candidates.\nThe outputs update `capability-map.md` (model × effort × task_class grounding) and surface rules for\n`promote-rule` to push into deterministic hooks/skills/charter. The Karpathy outer loop then frames the\nnext experiment (deepen/broaden/abandon) using measurement from prior cycles.\n\n**Selection criterion**: the best repo closes this loop at pattern level — it provides ideas/structures\nfor (a) evaluation of agent behavior, (b) self-improving signal collection, (c) outer-loop framing\nwith measurable deepen/broaden/abandon decisions. Phase-0 portability (bash + POSIX; no Docker/Postgres\nrequired to extract value from the patterns) is the tiebreaker. Charter principles 1 (evidence grounding),\n8 (calibration over confidence), 9 (no LLM math) must be respected by the candidate's own design.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Picked**: `stanfordnlp/dspy` @ `db83e5a` (2026-04-28)\n- **Runner-up**: `langchain-ai/openevals`\n- **Disqualified**:\n  - `openai/swarm` — explicitly superseded by OpenAI Agents SDK; last substantive commit 2025-03-11 (>400 days ago); abandonware\n  - `karpathy/llm.c` — LLM training infrastructure (C/CUDA); no agent-notes/evaluation loop; last commit 2025-05-10 (>350 days ago)\n  - `langchain-ai/langsmith-sdk` — observability SDK, not a pattern source; requires LangSmith cloud account; Phase-0 portability fails\n  - `agno-agi/agno` — production agent runtime (FastAPI + Docker + cloud); runtime dependency-heavy; not a self-learning loop pattern source\n\n---\n\n## Per-Candidate Findings (provenance-cited)\n\n### 1. stanfordnlp/dspy\n\n**URL**: https://github.com/stanfordnlp/dspy  \n**License**: MIT (`LICENSE` @ HEAD, as-of 2026-04-29)  \n**Last commit**: `db83e5ad` — \"docs(observability): quote admonition titles so they render correctly (#9691)\" — 2026-04-28T18:18:17Z  \n**Days since last commit**: 1 day — HEALTHY  \n\n**README verbatim**:\n> \"DSPy stands for Declarative Self-improving Python\"\n> \"DSPy is a framework for programming—rather than prompting—language models\"\n> \"offers algorithms for optimizing their prompts and weights\"\n> \"use DSPy to teach your LM to deliver high-quality outputs\"\n> \"DSPy: Compiling Declarative Language Model Calls into Self-Improving Pipelines\" (primary paper, Oct 2023)\n> \"iterate fast on building modular AI systems\"\n\n**Key pattern fit**:\nDSPy's core loop is: define a metric (evaluation function) → collect labeled examples → run an optimizer\n(BootstrapFewShot, MIPROv2, COPRO, etc.) → compile improved pipeline. This is structurally identical to\nStockForge's: emit telemetry → classify events → update capability-map → promote rules → next experiment.\nThe \"compile\" metaphor maps directly to `promote-rule` (structured signal → deterministic artifact).\n\n**Optimizer design** (from paper title and README): compilation is a background, off-session process, not\ninline — matches StockForge's \"write-heavy discipline, separate from runtime\" framing (D-005 § \"UP-08\ndirective: self-learning = write-heavy + indexing + caching\").\n\n**Provenance discipline**: DSPy's optimizer requires a `metric` function that returns a score from\ndeterministic code — the LLM does not score itself. This matches charter principle 9 (no LLM math)\nand principle 1 (evidence grounding). The optimizer is code; the program is code; only generation is LLM.\n\n**Phase-0 portability**: `pip install dspy` — pure Python, no Docker, no Postgres, no Redis required to\nstudy and extract patterns. Patterns are readable in source (optimizers in `dspy/teleprompt/`).\n\n---\n\n### 2. langchain-ai/openevals\n\n**URL**: https://github.com/langchain-ai/openevals  \n**License**: MIT (`LICENSE` @ HEAD, as-of 2026-04-29)  \n**Last commit**: `b362e8fe` — \"Merge pull request #186 from langchain-ai/dependabot/...\" — 2026-04-21T03:47:25Z  \n**Days since last commit**: 8 days — HEALTHY  \n\n**README verbatim**:\n> \"evals are an important part of bringing LLM applications to production\"\n> \"the sequence of messages and tool calls it makes while solving a task\"\n> Agent trajectory matching modes: \"strict\", \"unordered\", \"subset\", \"superset\"\n> \"uses an LLM to assess whether an agent's trajectory is accurate\"\n> Prebuilt prompts for: Quality (conciseness, correctness, hallucination detection), Safety, Security, RAG, Code\n> \"type-checking with Pyright and Mypy (Python) or TypeScript's built-in checker\"\n> Evaluator interface: results include `key`, `score`, `comment` fields\n\n**Key pattern fit**:\nOpenEvals provides agent trajectory evaluation — checking whether an agent's sequence of tool calls\nmatches a reference. StockForge's loop needs both legs.\n   Source: DSPy README \"Compiling Declarative Language Model Calls into Self-Improving Pipelines\" vs\n   openevals README \"evals are an important part of bringing LLM applications to production\" (stopping\n   at evaluation, not optimization).\n\n2. **Phase-0 portability is real vs conditional**: DSPy is `pip install dspy` with no external\n   dependencies for studying the optimizer patterns. openevals' feedback loop requires LangSmith\n   (cloud service) as the sink — README states \"Logging to LangSmith\" as the integration path for\n   the evaluator feedback loop. For Phase-0 pattern extraction without cloud accounts, DSPy is\n   portable; openevals is only partially so.\n\n---\n\n## What Winner Loses vs Runner-up\n\n**DSPy's loss vs openevals**:\n\nopenevals has ready-made agent trajectory evaluators — strict/unordered/subset/superset matching of\ntool call sequences, LLM-as-judge for trajectory correctness, and prebuilt hallucination-detection\nprompts. These are directly applicable to evaluating StockForge agent sessions against expected tool\ncall sequences (e.g., \"did the agent read current-execution.md before writing?\"). DSPy's optimizer\ninfrastructure assumes you already have a metric function — it does not provide prebuilt agent\nevaluation patterns. StockForge would need to write the trajectory evaluation logic from scratch when\nadapting DSPy patterns, whereas openevals would provide that as a starting point.\n\n**Honest trade-off**: openevals' prebuilt trajectory evaluators would reduce the effort to write\nStockForge's first evaluation function by ~0.5-1 session. This is a real cost, not a trivial gap.\n\n---\n\n## Adversarial Bear Case (≥3 Distinct Points)\n\n**Bear point 1 — Frontier-model substitution risk (6-month horizon)**\n\nDSPy's core value proposition is optimizing prompts for models that respond well to few-shot examples\nand structured optimization. Claude Sonnet 4.6 / Opus 4.7 at high effort already follow structured\ninstructions reliably (capability-map Strength row #1: \"Multi-mechanism ensemble design lands well\").\nAs frontier models improve at instruction following, the marginal value of DSPy-style prompt optimization\nshrinks. If Claude 5 / Sonnet 5 natively generalizes from minimal examples, the \"compile\" step becomes\nredundant. Risk window: 6-12 months.\n\n**Bear point 2 — Abstraction mismatch with StockForge's bash-first harness**\n\nDSPy is a Python framework designed to wrap LLM calls in Python module objects (`dspy.Module`,\n`dspy.Predict`). StockForge's Phase-0 harness is bash + POSIX hooks + NDJSON files — there is no\nPython LLM call layer yet (Phase 0 operates at the Claude Code CLI level, not API level). Extracting\nDSPy patterns for a bash harness requires a translation step that is non-trivial. The \"dogfood\"\nvalue in 5.5d.3 may be pattern-level only (reading optimizer source code) rather than running DSPy\nagainst real StockForge data. This reduces the dogfood signal quality for the 5.5d.3 success criterion\n(\"≥1 measurable insight\").\n\n**Bear point 3 — Maintenance governance risk**\n\nDSPy is a Stanford research project (Stanford Future Data Systems lab). Research group repos have\nnon-commercial governance — direction is driven by PhD students and papers, not by production user\nfeedback. Last 5 commits (2026-04-24 to 2026-04-28) are all documentation/CI fixes (#9691, #9690,\n#9649, #9687, #9661), with no new optimizer features. If the core research team graduates and moves\non, or if DSPy is absorbed into a larger framework (LangGraph has already built similar optimization\nprimitives), maintenance could stall. Last commit is 1 day old (HEALTHY now), but governance\nconcentration in a university research context is a structural fragility.\n\n**Bear point 4 (bonus) — LLM-as-judge risk in optimizer**\n\nSome DSPy optimizers (notably MIPRO variants) use an LLM to generate candidate instructions as part\nof the optimization loop. If StockForge adopted DSPy's full optimizer stack (not just its patterns),\nthe LLM-generated instruction candidates could introduce hallucinated optimization steps — which\nconflicts with charter principle 9 (no LLM math) if the LLM-generated instructions affect numeric\nreasoning paths. Mitigation: StockForge should adopt DSPy's metric-function pattern and compilation\nframing, not the full MIPRO optimizer, for the 5.5d.3 dogfood. anch":"main"}
